#113900 359 2005-11-11T19:31:31Z Melchoir79
Quote: crappitrash, Nov 11 2005, 01:03 PM
How do you aim?

I look at the position of my jump's dust plume to determine any mid-air corrections. It's generally better to err on the side of jumping too far, so you can hang back and control where you land. Also, although you're technically trying to hit the corner, if you bounce off the corner appreciably, your jump is much too short to get the corner jump.

Look at his highscores: http://www.harveycartel.org/metanet/n/data...u.php?name=naem . Not all of them are 0ths, of course. #127757 428 2005-12-28T23:16:51Z naem
You're certainly entitled to your opinion. I agree that I've sort of come out of nowhere with a fair amount of 0ths, but I've been spending a lot of time playing; not over a long period of time of course like some people have played for years, but since school has been out I've spent a lot of time playing N.

I have a frame of mind that won't let me be happy until my runs do look very nice. When I get top score on a level I am still disheartened by obvious mistakes - for example, it just pains me to watch 42-3 now because of the newbie mistake at the end. I like to spend hours on end on one particular level to make it look great, as opposed to taking as many levels as I can and just leaving them "good enough" to hold the 0th. There are a few exceptions, really hard maps for me that I know I won't ever perfect, but for a lot of the small to medium sized maps I don't really rest until they're near-perfect. 90-3 is one of those examples. It's a very short map and the only hard part about it is the ascent. I don't see what the big deal is, I'm not even touching my arrow keys at the beginning, just hitting my shift keys.

I'm sure there are ways to hack the game, as Skywing/trib5l has already pointed out. I don't pretend to know much about the heart of the game, but the hacker himself said (I'm paraphrasing) "it's not worth it to hack the demo data, it would take so long you are better off just playing the level." While there will always be some ambiguity towards the situation seeing as how I can't prove my legitimacy and there isn't a way to disprove it either - I think if you try my technique of using both shift keys in rapid succession to make jump combos, your anxieties may be eased a bit. It's a cool little trick and if you practice it enough you can get your jumps down to a near-fbf basis.

Also, I like to think that my success in a lot of the levels I've played isn't due to my speed at walljumping, but that it stems more from the new routes I come up with. Argue with my highscoes all you want, but no amount of sol hacking can reward you with a more efficient route in a map.

I find it a little humorous that you say you're SURE I'm cheating and then a few paragraphs later you intimate it as merely an inclination and that you may be wrong.

Frankly, I don't think I have to be a genius or a cheater, good brains don't equate to good reflexes. But then again you won't see me argue against anyone who wants to call me a genius

I dissagree that being innovative has nothing to do with being a cheater. You seem to be using a different definition of "cheating" to me. When I say so and so is cheating, I'm not saying that they are crappy players or aren't innovative. I simply mean that they are not playing how the rest of us are: they are using programs to slow down time, splicing demos, or whatever else, and as such are being dishonest when submitting scores achieved this way.

Regardless of whether kryx or naem have cheated, they are both obviously creative players, having discovered many new routes in various levels. That has nothing to do with them allegedly submitting scores that were obtained in a game environment that makes it easier to react very quickly. Nothing to do with cheating.

#186297 717 2006-06-08T17:20:41Z Brett_Sully_
Slowing down the frame rate opens up a whole new world of possibilities. I don't respect any innovation that has been cheated and passed off as legit. The innovations may seem great but are most likely to be incredibly easy when you slow down the frame rate, which is why they shouldn't be praised at all.
#186358 718 2006-06-08T19:07:22Z blobglob
Quote: Brett_Sully_, 2006-06-08T17:20:41Z
Slowing down the frame rate opens up a whole new world of possibilities. I don't respect any innovation that has been cheated and passed off as legit. The innovations may seem great but are most likely to be incredibly easy when you slow down the frame rate, which is why they shouldn't be praised at all.
I think there are two distinct skills involved in playing N (well, more like being a competitive high scorer). The first is being able to pull off fast runs on levels by having quick reactions and familiarity with the physics and controls, as well as a decent amount of patience. The second is being able to spot ways to optimise and improve on an existing route, or thinking up a new one altogether.

I think that while cheating makes the first of these skills almost irrelevant, the second is still as necessary as ever. It just tilts the balance further towards being a mind game rather than a mixture between that and a reaction game. The complaint about it is that scores obtained by cheating aren't a fair comparison to real-time scores.

#203375 873 2006-07-16T12:19:38Z clux
Quote: johnny_faneca, July 16, 2006 11:39 am
Key maping is a "real" problem as well. And it's different to have double shifting or, for example, z and x where you only need to use one hand. Besides you're choosing an option not included in the game which is, the way I see it, cheating.

but if you put your left hand on the biggest shift key, the result would be just the same, no? Consider this; double shifting is extremely troublesome to do well because of the insane coordination required between the left and right hand, and the other alternative; using the left hand on a big key (or two next two each other) is difficult as well because you don't have that kind of coordination in both hands. Two fingers on the right shift usually works great for most people because they're right handed, but when you use your right hand to control N, you're forced to switch between jump and ctrl, as I did in my 32-1 and and this.

No matter how you do it, with keymapping or whatever, you're left with these problems for every setup - unless you're ambidexterous - and I don't see how it does anything more than compensate for your personal inclinations or awkward key placement or whatever. Technically, it isn't any different from binding keys in a fps game in the console, just without a console : )

#308203 1076 2007-03-27T22:45:14Z Hendor
As far as I know, there are three ways of viewing highscores:

1 - In the game in the highscores menu that you select from the main menu. Just click an episode and a level and wait for the list to dowload. Then click a person's name and you c an watch their demo.

2 - NHigh. This is jg9000's program. It is an external program that downloads the scores from the metanet servers. Besides seeing the rankings for the levels, you can view many neat reports, like how many scores a certain person has, or how many 0ths a person has. Very useful. Find it here.

3 - The metanet site. Not really very useful. I never really use it except when I'm at a computer that doesn't have N or NHigh. Find them here

Hope I've been helpful.

#419277 1206 2008-03-20T21:22:55Z Pepi
Wait, what are "key mapping" and "macroing", exactly?
#419281 1207 2008-03-20T21:26:09Z darkshadow1416
Key mapping is changing the input of one key so the os recognizes it as another key. Macroing is the same thing but instead of changing it to another key it changes it to a sequence of inputs. Such as:
Shift
wait .025
Shift
which would allow you to do some incredibly hard jumps with no effort required.
#419283 1208 2008-03-20T21:31:58Z Pepi
Macroing seems like it would be very hard to control, because you'd have to set that up and then pause the game once you did the jump, right? Eh. I don't understand how key mapping could help you.
#419308 1209 2008-03-20T22:06:48Z darkshadow1416
Not quite. What you do is to set it up so a key like z for example would do the above macro that way when you needed two jumps in quick succession you would press z instead of pressing shift twice.
